# Skills · R · part 10

845 entries, ramo-explain to rank-sum-statistical-testing. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **ramo-explain**: Report-time explanation layer — turns a finished artifact (plan, verification report, diagnosis) into a four-part account a reader can act on, before any score or statistic is printed. Called by a pipeline skill at its report step; spawns nothing. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-explain) · ★30)
- **ramo-interview**: DS problem-definition interview — silently pre-reads context, asks up to 12 questions across 6 DS dimensions, saves to .ramo/interviews/, ends with an explicit baton to /ramo:eda or /ramo:plan. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-interview) · ★30)
- **ramo-mr**: Open a merge request (GitHub pull request or GitLab merge request, per vcs.provider) for the code an experiment produced — preflight, verify-PASS gate, MR body assembled from the plan and the cycle's own outputs, single creation call. Zero Agent() spawns. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-mr) · ★30)
- **ramo-plan**: DS experiment plan authoring — explore, write (plan-mode branch), parallel multi-review, main-session consensus, bounded improve loop, and an explicit baton to run. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-plan) · ★30)
- **ramo-research**: Pre-interview research — when a modeling problem needs what a codebase or the field already knows: discover sources, let the user curate and split them into topics, read through ramo-research-reader, and write one short, picture-first README per topic under docs/research/&lt;topic&gt;/ that the interview reads as background. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-research) · ★30)
- **ramo-run**: Execute an approved plan — preflight the external dependencies, parse the frozen 6-column Execution Structure, materialize .ramo/todo/, delegate each task once to its domain agent, print the two execution gates and decide the query gate by three machine checks, stop a person only for the closed stop set, enforce the envelope + evidence gates, retry on machine values only. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-run) · ★30)
- **ramo-verify**: Post-implementation verification — preflight and state precedence, five deterministic gates (the third re-runs every row's acceptance check and recomputes its hashes), a per-row fidelity matrix from ramo-critique, validity checklists from the domain verifiers, audit cells, synthesis without votes, a closed stop set, and a bounded Fix-TODO loop via domain implement agents. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-verify) · ★30)
- **ramo-wiki**: Author and publish a wiki lesson, constraint, note, or pipeline overview through the transaction gate — draft, deterministic validation, semantic review, commit. Invoked by ramo-doc's promotion triggers or directly when a caller already has a candidate fact to record. ([woowabros/ramo-harness](https://github.com/woowabros/ramo-harness/tree/HEAD/harness/skills/ramo-wiki) · ★30)

([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/quantum/random-dimension-reduction-quantum-learning) · ★4)
- **random-domain-knowledge-row-generator**: Reference documentation for the RandomDomainKnowledgeRowGenerator operator. [Purpose] Calls an LLM repeatedly with the same domain-generation prompt and writes the generated results into one column of an existing DataFrame. [When to use] Use it when you already have a seed DataFrame with the exact target row count and want to fill one output column with domain-specific generated content. If you need to build prompts from existing row fields, use PromptedGenerator or FormatStrPromptedGenerator instead. ([OpenDCAI/DataFlow-WebUI](https://github.com/OpenDCAI/DataFlow-WebUI/tree/HEAD/skills/canonical/core_text/generate/random-domain-knowledge-row-generator) · ★257 · 3 copies)
- **random-draw**: AISP-backed bridge for 本地等概率随机抽签, 从用户给定的多个对等选项中无偏随机抽取一个(非严肃决策建议)。. Use when 在多个对等选项间需要无偏随机抽取一个时; 随机抽签/抓阄/多选一随机抉择. Do not use when 医疗/法律/财务等严肃或高风险决策; 需要按权重/概率不等的加权抽取. ([AIXP-Labs/SoulAgent](https://github.com/AIXP-Labs/SoulAgent/tree/HEAD/claude_code_plugin/skills/run/soulagent/aisp/random_draw_aisp) · 3 copies)
- **random-effects-heterogeneity-estimation**: Use when the same gene pairs have Pearson correlation coefficients measured in multiple studies, each with its own sample size, and you need pooled fixed and random effects estimates with a heterogeneity statistic. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-effects-heterogeneity-estimation))
- **random-effects-meta-analysis**: Use when combining K&gt;=10 studies across multiple related markers (e.g., 6 alpha-diversity indices) from summary statistics only — effect estimates and standard errors — with no prior correlation or full-data access. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-effects-meta-analysis))
- **random-effects-meta-analysis-of-taxa-estimates**: Use when you have comparable per-study differential taxa comparison results (estimates + standard errors from GAMLSS-BEZI taxa.compare) from ≥2 microbiome studies and need pooled overall effects and heterogeneity across studies. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-effects-meta-analysis-of-taxa-estimates))
- **random-effects-meta-analysis-pvalue-computation**: Use when you have study-specific regression coefficients and standard errors (effect estimates + SEs) for ≥10 studies across multiple related markers, and need p-values that avoid the over-conservatism of traditional random effects meta-analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/random-effects-meta-analysis-pvalue-computation))
- **random-effects-model-specification-methylation**: Decision support for choosing among MethylCal's four random-effects model specifications (equations 2-5) for a methylation calibration assay when standard-control data at known methylation percentages are available and goodness-of-fit comparison via DIC is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-effects-model-specification-methylation))
- **random-effects-simulation-design**: Use when you need simulated zero-inflated negative-binomial count data with known random-effect variance components (sigma.a, sigma.b) to benchmark mixed-effects differential expression methods like TWO-SIGMA or MAST. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-effects-simulation-design))
- **random-encounter**: Generate random encounters appropriate for a location, party level, or situation. Creates combat, social, or exploration encounters using existing world entities. Use when user wants "random encounter", "encounter table", or "what happens at [location]". ([hopeoverture/Worldbuilding-System](https://github.com/hopeoverture/Worldbuilding-System/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/random-encounter) · ★7)
- **random-encounter**: Use AT THE TABLE for a random encounter roll. Reads ONLY the matching context/tables/ file, rolls the die, and reports the encounter line + 1 short flavor note. Does NOT design encounters. Does NOT scale them. Does NOT write files. ([Jikodis/dungeon-master](https://github.com/Jikodis/dungeon-master/tree/HEAD/.agents/skills/live/random-encounter))
- **random-encounter-creator**: DEPRECATED — superseded by urban-d100-encounters (same plugin), kept one MINOR release for compatibility. Do not trigger for new work; route to urban-d100-encounters. This skill generated d100-d1000 random encounter tables for dark fantasy cities across a six-category taxonomy (Social, Danger, Mystery, Horror, Environmental, Event); all of its references and its generation script have been repatriated to urban-d100-encounters, which uses an 11-theme taxonomy and named NPCs. ([camauger/ludomancien-skills](https://github.com/camauger/ludomancien-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/ttrpg-tables/skills/random-encounter-creator) · ★2)
- **random-entry**: Random word/concept as thinking entry point (de Bono Random Entry) ([yogsoth-ai/creative-ideation](https://github.com/yogsoth-ai/creative-ideation/tree/HEAD/skills/random-entry) · ★3)
- **random-exonic-snv-simulation**: Use when you need to quantify how much of the scRNA-seq SNV recovery rate is due to read depth vs. caller bias, by simulating known variants in aligned reads. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-exonic-snv-simulation))
- **random-facts**: Get random interesting and useless facts using the Useless Facts API. ([DhruvitNavadiya/market-place](https://github.com/DhruvitNavadiya/market-place/tree/HEAD/app/agents/free/random-facts) · ★3)
- **random-forest-advanced**: Use when implementing advanced random forest models. ([LoopyLuci/Skills](https://github.com/LoopyLuci/Skills/tree/HEAD/skills/random-forest-advanced) · ★2)
- **random-forest-binding-prediction**: Use when the goal is to predict or reproduce TF-DNA binding affinities from experimental intensity/enrichment data (gcPBM, uPBM, HT-SELEX) using structural/mechanical DNA features rather than sequence-only models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-binding-prediction))
- **random-forest-biomarker-prediction**: Use when shotgun metagenomes have been profiled with MetaPhlAn 4 SGBs and the goal is to predict host health/dietary markers or quantify how uncharacterized taxa improve prediction over a prior profile version. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/random-forest-biomarker-prediction))
- **random-forest-cell-compartment-segmentation**: Use when segmenting single cells from multiplexed imaging data (e.g., MIBI or multiplexed immunofluorescence tissue images such as colorectal adenoma or breast carcinoma TMA stacks) where membranes are incomplete and per-cell compartment intensities are needed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-cell-compartment-segmentation))
- **random-forest-cell-type-classification**: Use when comparing imputation or normalization methods on labeled single-cell RNA-seq expression matrices and you need a classifier-based metric (out-of-bag error) of cell-type separation before vs after imputation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cell-type-classification))
- **random-forest-cell-type-classification-on-embeddings**: Use when labeled single-cell data (e.g. BMMC cells with 14 manually gated populations) has been embedded with ivis and you need a quantitative accuracy readout of the embedding, especially for held-out cells mapped with the parametric transform. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cell-type-classification-on-embeddings))
- **random-forest-classification**: Use when metabolomics or proteomics feature tables (CSV features + target/metadata files, e.g. ST000284 or ST000336) need exploratory visualization and quality checks before statistical analysis. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classification))
- **random-forest-classification**: Use when designing a multi-omic classification experiment and you need to know how many samples a random forest predictor needs to achieve a fixed target error rate across single- and multi-omic feature combinations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/random-forest-classification))
- **random-forest-classification-evaluation**: Use when comparing filter FS algorithms on tabular data (data matrix + response): each algorithm's ranked feature list must be translated into downstream RF out-of-sample misclassification percentages to judge which selections generalize. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classification-evaluation))
- **random-forest-classification-for-metabolite-prediction**: Use when you have a metabolomics count table (rows=metabolites, columns=samples) with associated metadata containing a categorical grouping factor (e. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classification-for-metabolite-prediction) · 2 copies)
- **random-forest-classifier-application**: Use when genomic bins must be classified as active/primed enhancers from ChIP-seq histone marks and DNase-seq accessibility data, e.g. reproducing enhancer discovery in human cell lines like HCT116 or HeLa. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-application))
- **random-forest-classifier-benchmarking**: Use when gene-expression data from CRC pre-clinical models (cell lines, organoids, PDXs) needs CMS1-CMS4 subtype calls, and you must quantify how the original primary-CRC classifier's stromal/immune signal dependency leaves samples (notably CMS4) unclassified. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-benchmarking))
- **random-forest-classifier-inference**: Use when you have a gene-cluster absence/presence matrix for genomes labeled 'unknown' and literature-derived lifestyle metadata plus trained binary random forest classifiers (scikit-learn, n_estimators = 50) to predict a lifestyle per genome at &gt;0.8 confidence. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-inference))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when you have a raw NanoSV (or similar long-read) SV call set with per-call features and an independent benchmark truth set (e.g. 1KG or PB SV sites) and need a principled, stringent post-calling filter instead of hand-tuned cutoffs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--2f5ed41f8e6718dc))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when you have aligned PacBio CCS reads with kinetic tags and need a baseline supervised classifier to test whether kinetic signals discriminate two base states and to rank feature contributions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--07fcf1197d6faaa1))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when a Deblur BIOM feature table with sample metadata (e.g. EMPO level 2/3 labels) is available and the goal is supervised, cross-validated classification of samples into environment categories from abundance data alone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when a metabolomics intensity matrix (e.g., 234 samples, 113 metabolites, 3 phenotype groups) must be split into train/test sets, modeled with random forest, and validated via ROC AUC against a held-out test set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--d61ad68b5824fa1e))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when you have experimental EI-MS or ESI-MS/MS spectra of labeled bitter/non-bitter compounds and need to train, select, and evaluate a Random Forest against XGBoost and a naive baseline. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--4975d1ebd82b5842))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when a labeled, high-dimensional lipid feature table (e.g., annotated by LipidIN, such as Supplementary Data 9 breast cancer cohorts) needs feature ranking and biomarker panel selection for patient-vs-healthy classification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--8883a20cf751edd3))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when a quality-controlled LC-MS feature table with sample metadata (e.g. age group, sex, brain region) is available and the goal is metabolome-informed phenotype prediction via a random forest with feature selection and ROC-AUC / cross-validation evaluation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--728f93a1a0de83da))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when a gene-by-cell scRNA-seq count matrix with trusted per-cell labels (cancer, immune, stromal) is available and the goal is to train a hierarchical supervised classifier or benchmark it via stratified cross-validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--e42e97b607f64a29))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when assessing whether augmenting a sparse or downsampled scRNA-seq cell population (e.g., cluster 2 from Fresh 68k PBMCs) improves downstream classification robustness, with F1 score as the metric. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--dfaf904e5e82a2bb))
- **random-forest-classifier-training**: Use when you have PacBio long-read RNA-seq BAM data processed by dsRID into positive (dsRID_train.tsv) and negative (dsRID_null.tsv) dsRNA region tables and need to train and validate a classifier that discriminates candidate dsRNA regions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-classifier-training--7a66a68f700c3912))
- **random-forest-classifier-tuning**: Use when a binary-classification outcome is being predicted from pathway-transformed multi-omics abundance data (e.g. Su et al. COVID-19 mild vs moderate/severe metabolomics/proteomics scores) and an interpretable pathway-level model with feature selection is required. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-classifier-tuning))
- **random-forest-consensus-model-training**: Use when you have per-read methylation score files (ID/Chr/Pos/Strand/Score columns) from two or more nanopore CpG methylation tools run on the same reads, and need a consensus per-read methylation probability more accurate than either tool alone. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-consensus-model-training))
- **random-forest-cross-validated-classification**: Use when you need a supervised classification baseline on peak-aligned metabolomics feature tables (e.g., cosine-similarity or spectral-entropy similarity tables) with labeled bioactivity groups, reported as accuracy, precision, recall, F1, and ROC AUC. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-cross-validated-classification))
- **random-forest-cross-validated-hyperparameter-tuning**: Use when training a supervised loop-detection model from a 10 kb Hi-C cooler file and a bedpe positive set (e.g., H3K27ac HiChIP), needing per-chromosome models and tuned hyperparameters before scoring pixels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validated-hyperparameter-tuning))
- **random-forest-cross-validation**: Use when a variant prioritization pipeline needs an ex-ante pathogenicity probability per coding variant computed only from genomic and evolutionary annotations (allele frequency, conservation, deleteriousness predictors), before any phenotype or inheritance information is used. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation))
- **random-forest-cross-validation-classification**: Use when benchmarking how well cell-cell communication features (tensor factor loadings or UMAP embeddings from LR communication scores) separate biological phenotypes such as COVID-19 status and severity. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation-classification))
- **random-forest-cross-validation-evaluation**: Use when comparing feature subsets or classifiers for DNA 6mA sequence prediction on 41nt sequences and you need cross-validated AUC estimates (e.g. Binary vs Motif+Kmer+Binary) before freezing a feature matrix. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-cross-validation-evaluation))
- **random-forest-discrimination-auc**: Decision support for quantifying how realistic synthetic scRNA-seq cells are: train a random forest on real-vs-generated labels and use the resulting AUC, where values near 0.5 (positive control ~0.52) indicate generated cells are indistinguishable from real PBMC cells. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-discrimination-auc))
- **random-forest-dna-affinity-regression**: Use when predicting TF binding affinities from uPBM (35-mer oligos), gcPBM, or HT-SELEX datasets with a random forest over MD-derived DNA shape/flexibility features rather than sequence-only CNNs like DeepBind. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-dna-affinity-regression))
- **random-forest-driver-gene-classification**: Use when classifying unlabelled cancer genes into TSGs/OGs from somatic mutation feature matrices (37 ratio-metric/entropy features), e.g. pan-cancer or tissue-specific driver gene prediction with CGC training labels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-driver-gene-classification))
- **random-forest-enhancer-classification**: Use when labeled enhancer and promoter (non-enhancer) genomic element sets are available with ChIP-seq and DNase-seq signal tracks (e.g. RPM-normalized bedGraph) and the goal is to train, validate, or apply a supervised enhancer-prediction model. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-enhancer-classification))
- **random-forest-ensemble**: Multi-surface random forest ensemble with Python surface for bagged tree training and SQLite surface for member tracking and ensemble prediction aggregation. Supports configurable tree counts. ([SpectreDeath/Em-Cubed](https://github.com/SpectreDeath/Em-Cubed/tree/HEAD/skills/MACHINE_LEARNING/random-forest-ensemble))
- **random-forest-ensemble-inference**: Use when a small labelled gene set (e.g. 136 TSGs and 76 OGs from CGC) must be extended to unlabelled genes using somatic mutation-derived features, and consensus across ensemble fold models is needed to reduce overfitting. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-ensemble-inference))
- **random-forest-ensemble-training**: Use when classifying candidate intronic terminal exons (or similar genomic regions) from conventional RNA-seq alignments, needing a supervised model with region-level features and held-out AUROC-style validation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-ensemble-training))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-analysis**: Use when a trained random forest (e.g. scikit-learn) combines multiple candidate-scoring features and you must determine which feature contributes most to ranking performance. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-analysis))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-extraction**: Use when comparing how training data source (uPBM vs gcPBM vs HT-SELEX) changes the relative importance of DNA feature classes in the DNAffinity random forest regressor, or when auditing which physical parameters drive affinity predictions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-extraction))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-extraction**: Use when trained random forest regression models (e.g. per-metabolite models over CCLE multiomics features) need their feature importances ranked, top-k features extracted, and robustness-confidence scores computed. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-extraction))
- **random-forest-feature-importance-ranking**: Use when a small, class-imbalanced labelled gene set (e.g. 136 TSGs vs 76 OGs) must be ranked by which mutation-type features (entropy, frequency, ratio-metric) drive random forest classification, avoiding single-model importance instability. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-feature-importance-ranking))
- **random-forest-gene-importance-ranking**: Use when a log-normalized scRNA-seq expression matrix with initial cluster labels needs a supervised, label-aware gene ranking (HIG) to complement unsupervised HVG selection before rare cell type detection. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-gene-importance-ranking))
- **random-forest-gradient-boosting-classification**: Use when classifying single-cell RNA-seq cells from CKG or gene expression feature matrices with supervised tree-based models, or when RF/GBM feature_importances_ rankings are needed to compare feature representations. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-gradient-boosting-classification))
- **random-forest-hyperparameter-tuning**: Use when classifying genes as TSGs vs OGs from a pre-processed mutation feature matrix (e.g. FeatureMat.tsv with 37 ratio-metric/entropy features) with CGC labels, and stable hyper-parameters are needed to avoid overfitting on a small labelled set. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-hyperparameter-tuning))
- **random-forest-iterative-imputation**: Use when an n x p matrix with 10–30% of values removed completely at random contains mixed variable types (continuous + categorical) and a non-parametric imputation is needed, or when nonlinear relations/complex interactions are suspected. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-iterative-imputation))
- **random-forest-learning-curve-analysis**: Use when you must show how a spectral classifier's precision and recall change as the training set is grown from small to full size, e.g. to justify a data-collection effort or reproduce a published learning curve. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-learning-curve-analysis))
- **random-forest-lifestyle-classification**: Use when gene cluster absence/presence matrices paired with literature-based lifestyle metadata are available and the goal is to classify held-out genomes' lifestyle, including leave-one-species-out validation for novel species. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-lifestyle-classification))
- **random-forest-matrix-completion**: Use when a preprocessed LC-MS MetaboSet object still contains NA values in exprs and a statistically sound fill-in (random forest, per Kokla et al.) is needed before feature-wise tests or multivariate models. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/random-forest-matrix-completion))

- **random-forest-missing-value-imputation**: Use when a behavioral item matrix (e.g. ASEBA CBCL parent-rated externalizing items) contains missing item responses and a completed matrix is required before factor extraction such as multiple correspondence analysis. - **random-forest-model-evaluation**: Use when a metabolomics feature table with phenotype labels (e.g. CRC, polyp, healthy controls) needs supervised classification and validation of predictive performance via random forest OOB error rates. - **random-forest-model-training**: Use when a two-class or multi-class phenotype classification model must be built from a merged MetaPhlAn table or QIIME/BIOM OTU table plus metadata (e.g. T1D t/f labels), and RF accuracy must be tuned and validated. - **random-forest-model-training-hic**: Use when a 10 kb-binned contact map (.cool or .hic) and a positive interaction list (bedpe from ChIA-PET, HiChIP, or annotated loops) are available and supervised chromatin loop detection is needed. - **random-forest-model-training-on-3c-derived-interactions**: Use when you have a 10 kb-binned .cool or .hic Hi-C matrix and a bedpe list of positive interactions (ChIA-PET, HiChIP, Capture Hi-C) and need chromosome-wise random forest models for loop prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-model-training-on-3c-derived-interactions))
- **random-forest-model-training-per-chromosome**: Use when you have a .cool or .hic genome-wide contact map and an orthogonal positive interaction set (e.g. HiChIP/ChIA-PET bedpe) and need supervised loop classifiers for genome-wide prediction. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-epigenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/epigenomics/v2/leaves/random-forest-model-training-per-chromosome))
- **random-forest-normal-cancer-classification**: Use when you need computational validation that gene communities extracted from fused networks are relevant, by testing whether they classify TCGA normal/cancer samples better than DE or random gene sets. - **random-forest-oob-error-estimation**: Use when running missForest on data with artificially or naturally missing values and you need an imputation error estimate without holding out a test set, e.g. to report NRMSE/PFC reliability per simulation run. - **random-forest-out-of-sample-classification-evaluation**: Use when you need a label-based, out-of-sample measure of embedding quality — e.g. scoring ivis embeddings of BMMC CyTOF subsamples with manually gated cell-population labels via held-out classification accuracy. - **random-forest-permutation-feature-importance**: Use when a random-forest model (e.g., dsRID trained on Pacbio_AD_data.tsv) needs per-feature contribution scores rather than generic impurity-based importance, to explain which molecular traits drive dsRNA region prediction. - **random-forest-phenotype-classification**: Use when classifying metagenomic samples into phenotype classes (e.g., T2D, cirrhosis, T1D) from merged MetaPhlAn or QIIME/BIOM taxonomic profile tables with matching tab-separated metadata, and a robust RF-based accuracy estimate is needed. - **random-forest-pixel-classification**: Use when segmenting cells from multiplexed imaging data (e.g., multiplexed immunofluorescence colorectal adenoma images or MIBI breast carcinoma images) and you need pixel-level classification of cellular compartments to seed membrane-based resegmentation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/rank-selection-by-reconstruction-error))
- **rank-shortlist**: Produce a ranked shortlist of people for a Workerbee role. Use when a customer says "rank them", "who are the top candidates?", "run the matching", "give me a shortlist", "score the applicants", or "who should I look at first?". Runs the live match_candidates tool (synchronous — returns the ranked list inline) and presents scannable ranked cards with one-line drivers; reads existing results via get_job_context. ([Workerbee-Inc/workerbee-marketplace](https://github.com/Workerbee-Inc/workerbee-marketplace/tree/HEAD/plugins/workerbee/skills/rank-shortlist))
- **rank-shortlist**: Produce a ranked shortlist of people for a Workerbee role. Use when a customer says "rank them", "who are the top candidates?", "run the matching", "give me a shortlist", "score the applicants", or "who should I look at first?". Runs the live match_candidates tool (synchronous — returns the ranked list inline) and presents scannable ranked cards with one-line drivers; reads existing results via get_job_context. Reads skillCounts from get_success_profile to tell whether the standard behind the ranking is still the broad base occupation graph, and offers build-success-profile's Tailor step when… ([Workerbee-Inc/workerbee-marketplace-staging](https://github.com/Workerbee-Inc/workerbee-marketplace-staging/tree/HEAD/plugins/workerbee/skills/rank-shortlist))
- **rank-sim-idea**: Triage a proposed Clowder simulation idea with the V×F×R×C×H rubric before writing code. Use whenever the user proposes adding a new system, feature, mechanic, creature, item, or behavior to the sim. Produces a score, bucket, and recommendation anchored against the shadowfox calibration — prevents narratively-cool, ops-expensive ideas from slipping in unpriced. Trigger phrases - "add X to the sim", "what if cats could Y", "new system for Z", "I want a feature that...", "we should have a &lt;creature/item/mechanic&gt;". Do NOT fire on balance tweaks to existing systems, bug fixes, or refactors. ([wn-mitch/clowder](https://github.com/wn-mitch/clowder/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/rank-sim-idea) · ★2 · 2 copies)
- **rank-software-decisions**: Ranks software engineering options with an anonymized, blog-derived value pattern that favors reversibility, visible hidden costs, and survivable failure modes. Use when ranking architecture choices, implementation approaches, build-vs-buy decisions, tooling choices, dependency changes, migration plans, reliability tradeoffs, or AI/agent workflow decisions. ([PeiyuanQi/work-like-me](https://github.com/PeiyuanQi/work-like-me/tree/HEAD/wlm/plugins/swe/skills/rank-software-decisions))
- **rank-stabilization-median-permutation**: Use when reproducing the VIBE benchmark (305 patient cases / 308 causal genes) or any gene-prioritization benchmark where the causal gene may be missing from tool output and its rank must be randomly assigned, requiring outlier-robust per-case rank aggregation. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/rank-stabilization-median-permutation))
- **rank-statistic-null-distribution-derivation**: Use when ranks of features (e.g. genes) are available from two or more independent replicate experiments and you need the theoretical null CDF of the maximum rank statistic to estimate pi1 or assign marginal FDRs to reproducible features. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/rank-statistic-null-distribution-derivation))
- **rank-statistic-simulation-generation**: Use when benchmarking a rank-based reproducibility method under realistic correlation decay — e.g., testing whether MaRR maintains FDR, power, and low bias when correlation between replicate pairs decreases with rank. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/rank-statistic-simulation-generation))
- **rank-sum-selection-scoring**: Use when you have both feature-wise test results with FDR-adjusted p-values and multivariate model (e.g., RF 'mid' model) importance ranks, and you need one ordered shortlist of robust features for metabolite identification. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/rank-sum-selection-scoring))
- **rank-sum-statistical-testing**: Use when two independent sets of scores must be compared for a significant shift — e.g. donor-pair NMI values from MuDCoD vs. PisCES at one time point — and a nonparametric test on unpaired samples is appropriate. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/rank-sum-statistical-testing))
